Route correctness-critical work here — concurrency, security-sensitive code, data migrations, algorithms, or anything costly to revert. Receives the standard five-part spec, implements the change itself (no external CLI), runs the verification, and returns a structured report with evidence. Same model family as the architect — use the grok lane when you want vendor diversity. ([huytdps13400/fable-advisor](https://github.com/huytdps13400/fable-advisor/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer-high.md) · ★1)
- **opus-implementer-high**: Opus implementation agent at high effort — the quota-friendly tier for S-sized, well-specified, mechanical parity packages (copy ports, option lists, small advisory branches, UI stubs). Same conventions as opus-implementer, leaner reasoning budget. ([Recon222/demo-website-dvr-extraction-notes](https://github.com/Recon222/demo-website-dvr-extraction-notes/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-implementer-high.md))
- **opus-implementer-low**: Implementation lane running Claude Opus at LOW reasoning effort. Route mechanical or boilerplate changes here where the spec fully determines the output — but prefer the grok lane for this class of work unless you specifically want a Claude producer. Receives the standard five-part spec, implements the change itself (no external CLI), runs the verification, and returns a structured report with evidence. ([huytdps13400/fable-advisor](https://github.com/huytdps13400/fable-advisor/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer-low.md) · ★1)
- **opus-implementer-max**: Opus implementation agent at MAX effort — for the subtler parity work packages assigned to Opus (nuanced UX ports, tricky state interactions, packages whose failure modes reviews catch late). Same conventions as opus-implementer, deeper reasoning budget. ([Recon222/demo-website-dvr-extraction-notes](https://github.com/Recon222/demo-website-dvr-extraction-notes/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-implementer-max.md))
- **opus-implementer-medium**: Implementation lane running Claude Opus at MEDIUM reasoning effort. Route non-trivial logic that isn't safety-critical here — standard feature code, moderate refactors. Receives the standard five-part spec, implements the change itself (no external CLI), runs the verification, and returns a structured report with evidence. Same model family as the architect — use the grok lane when you want vendor diversity. ([huytdps13400/fable-advisor](https://github.com/huytdps13400/fable-advisor/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-implementer-medium.md) · ★1)
- **opus-implementor**: Pane agent (herdr panes `opus-imple-&lt;n&gt;`, `claude --agent opus-implementor`): Opus implementer of one briefed Mado work package, test-first. Not for audits, plans, PRs, merges or device records. ([anzchy/mado](https://github.com/anzchy/mado/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-implementor.md) · ★1)
- **opus-janitor**: Council janitor. Runs last on captain's draft. Flags bloat, returns BLOAT_FLAG + CUTS list + WORD_COUNT. Never trims itself — captain removes. Runs on Opus 5 as a live teammate. ([ANcpLua/ancplua-claude-plugins](https://github.com/ANcpLua/ancplua-claude-plugins/blob/HEAD/plugins/council/agents/opus-janitor.md) · ★6)
- **opus-judge**: Judgement lane — a frontier model, for planning and final verdict/synthesis only. Opus 5.5 at max effort. Use in short bursts, two touches per feature max — a plan up front, or a verdict / cross-attempt synthesis / ship go-no-go at the end. Thinking, not typing — never a resident worker, never code-writing. ([reasance/delegation-kit](https://github.com/reasance/delegation-kit/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-judge.md))
- **opus-low**: Internal Kerd routing agent for Opus at low reasoning effort. Invoke only when Kerd Conductor or Agent explicitly selects kerd:opus-low for a delegated job; do not choose it for ordinary requests. The name carries the model and the effort so the running-job list shows both. The dispatching call still names the model (opus); this definition sets the same model, so a call that omits it still resolves to Opus, unless the host forces or restricts the subagent model. ([anthonymaley/Kerd](https://github.com/anthonymaley/Kerd/blob/HEAD/agents/opus-low.md) · ★3)

- **opus-low**: Delegated worker (Opus) at low effort, for a quick expert look at a small, fully supplied context where depth is not needed. Never for mechanical steps, which go to sonnet-low. ([bantoilatoi/AgentSkills](https://github.com/bantoilatoi/AgentSkills/blob/HEAD/setup/claude/agents/opus-low.md))
- **opus-low**: Router worker: Opus 5.5 at low effort. Started only by the router. ([Light505/gswitchtool](https://github.com/Light505/gswitchtool/blob/HEAD/integrations/router-plugin/agents/opus-low.md))
- **opus-low**: General-purpose worker running Opus at low reasoning effort. Not for architecture decisions, ambiguous specs, or subtle debugging. ([Sartonio/ai-first-starter](https://github.com/Sartonio/ai-first-starter/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/opus-low.md))

- **oracle-releaser**: Leaf agent. The last line before code meets production. - **orch-autopilot-planner**: The planner of one quick autopilot issue - runs the unattended interview, the done check, the label move, orch-to-spec's Unattended rewrite, the plan and autopilot start for exactly one issue, and returns one line, started, skipped or failed. - **orch-builder**: Build executor for orchestrator-mode. Use to execute build steps from an approved plan exactly as scoped. Surfaces any needed deviation instead of improvising. Never verifies its own work — that belongs to orch-checker. - **orch-builder**: Builder lane for the orchestrate workflow — implements ONE brief on ONE branch in its own worktree, red-then-green tests on changed files only, pushes, writes a report; never opens PRs or deploys. - **orch-builder**: Independent coding and execution worker. Use for a feature, a localised bug, tests, a refactor, a script, or a piece of research or analysis with a clear goal and a way to check the result. It plans its own approach, does the work, and verifies it. Prefer it when the task is well scoped and can be confirmed by running something. - **orch-checker**: Independent verifier for orchestrator-mode. Use to run a build step's done-test, both directions, after orch-builder finishes it. Never fixes anything; never the same agent that built. Read-only by design so it cannot quietly patch what it finds. ([joeoliveimpact/revxl-marketplace](https://github.com/joeoliveimpact/revxl-marketplace/blob/HEAD/plugins/promptception/agents/orch-checker.md) · ★1)
- **orch-classifier**: Classifies a user prompt into a V3 route-decision (tier S/P/T + technique). Dispatched when prompt-gate output is missing or ambiguous. Read-only. - **orch-classifier-specialist**: You are the classifier-routing pillar's primary specialist. - **orch-closer**: The closer of one orchestrator review loop - at termination, deduplicates and files the unfixed majors and nits across this loop's review records, posts the loop's one PR comment, writes the Filed list into the final record, and returns the issue numbers. - **orch-code-auditor**: The hunter of an orchestrator code audit - reads one area's source code and reports latent bugs, inefficiencies and architecture deepenings, each with the evidence its kind needs, writing its findings to one file. - **orch-planner**: Read-only Planner Agent for dev-orchestrator, on Opus. MUST BE USED by the dev-orchestrator whenever a complex dev task arrives without a Lead-provided plan, or the user asks to create, draft or design a plan ("crea un plan", "planifica", "plan this"). Investigates the codebase (graphify first when available) and returns a numbered, dependency-aware plan draft for the Lead to approve. Never edits files, never spawns agents, never talks to the Lead. ([Menendez2004/menendez-market](https://github.com/Menendez2004/menendez-market/blob/HEAD/plugins/skills/dev-orchestrator/agents/orch-planner.md) · ★2)
- **orch-planner**: Produces a plan.v3.json DAG from a route-decision. Reads context, declares node file sets, assigns roles, sets termination condition. Never edits files itself. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/.claude/plugins/00-orchestrator/agents/orch-planner.md) · ★1)
- **orch-planner**: Reach for this when work crosses several files or sittings and you cannot yet name the steps: it reads the ground and returns an ordered plan with a done-when. Not for a change you can already describe in three steps — just make it. ([1xmint/orchestrate](https://github.com/1xmint/orchestrate/blob/HEAD/skills/orchestrate/assets/agents/orch-planner.md))
- **orch-planner**: Architecture/planning worker for the orchestrator skill (L or high-risk tasks: design, decomposition, risk analysis). Use ONLY when the orchestrator dispatches a planning brief (model and effort passed per call). Not for direct use. ([czekanskyy/claude-orchestrator](https://github.com/czekanskyy/claude-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/plugins/orchestrator/agents/orch-planner.md))
- **orch-planner**: Le o ticket e o codigo real e escreve o plano de implementacao em PLAN.md, sem editar nada. Use antes de qualquer implementacao dentro de um worktree de ticket do orquestrador. ([jjr-dev/orchestrator-orca](https://github.com/jjr-dev/orchestrator-orca/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/orch-planner.md))
- **orch-premortem**: Adversarial reviewer for orchestrator-mode plan mode. Use for premortem legwork on a completed draft plan before it reaches the user — assumes the plan already failed and works backwards. Read-only; may re-probe claims live. The orchestrator owns the final verdict; this agent supplies the findings. ([joeoliveimpact/revxl-marketplace](https://github.com/joeoliveimpact/revxl-marketplace/blob/HEAD/plugins/promptception/agents/orch-premortem.md) · ★1)
- **orch-principal**: General worker for assignments that need substantial judgment. Use for substantive writing and editing, feature implementation across components, research synthesis, planning and architecture, difficult debugging, and integration work where ambiguity has to be resolved and one coherent deliverable produced. ([siddharthanagula3/agiworkforce](https://github.com/siddharthanagula3/agiworkforce/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/orch-principal.md))
- **orch-provider-renderer**: You are the install-saga pillar's provider renderer specialist. You edit: - `bundle/lib/providers/claude.mjs` (renderClaude, renderSettingsJson, renderRootMcpJson, applySurfaceLockdown) - `bundle/lib/providers/codex.mjs` (renderCodex, hardcoded MCP entries — §15-E) - `bundle/lib/providers/cursor.mjs` (renderCursor, hardcoded MCP entries — §15-F) - `bundle/lib/providers/codex-juror.mjs` (CLI counci ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_C/03-install-saga/agents/orch-provider-renderer.md) · ★1)
- **orch-provider-specialist**: Edit provider rendering (`claude.mjs`, `codex.mjs`, `cursor.mjs`), MCP registry, and surface lockdown. Use when changing provider templates. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/archive/inert-plugins-2026-05/01-install-surface/agents/orch-provider-specialist.md) · ★1)
- **orch-qa**: Senior QA/QC engineer. Framework-agnostic. Evaluates test quality across an existing codebase, triages failures, identifies missing test coverage, and writes missing tests when authorized. Produces evidence-backed quality reports. Use when the orchestrator (the /orch-qa skill, or another agent) needs the QA-engineer judgment — failure classification, lens-based gap analysis, test-writing for missing coverage. ([hideki5123/agent-skill-set](https://github.com/hideki5123/agent-skill-set/blob/HEAD/my-marketplace/plugins/orch-qa/agents/orch-qa.md) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **orch-reader**: Deep code reader for orchestrator-mode. Use for non-trivial code reads, cross-system translation (read a working implementation elsewhere and say what it means here), and PORT/WIRE verdicts with file:line citations. Read-only. A wrong port assessment is the most expensive mistake available — this agent exists so those verdicts come from read source, never from filenames. ([joeoliveimpact/revxl-marketplace](https://github.com/joeoliveimpact/revxl-marketplace/blob/HEAD/plugins/promptception/agents/orch-reader.md) · ★1)
- **orch-registry-cleanup-specialist**: Edit workshop registry, cleanup utility, smoke-cleanup, inventory script. Use for cross-project state changes. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/archive/inert-plugins-2026-05/04-tooling-surface/agents/orch-registry-cleanup-specialist.md) · ★1)
- **orch-researcher**: Two-pass repo research for one chunk: discovery by grep and glob, then targeted reads. ([Ramtoi/skill-tree](https://github.com/Ramtoi/skill-tree/blob/HEAD/tests/fixtures/ships_with/orchestrate-advanced/agents/orch-researcher.md) · ★5)
- **orch-researcher**: Use when research-classifier returns RESEARCH_NEEDED. Checks the planned approach against current sources and returns VERIFIED, COULDN'T_VERIFY, CONTRADICTED or NOT_APPLICABLE with a short brief. ([felipemelendez/llm-orchestrator](https://github.com/felipemelendez/llm-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-researcher.md) · ★3)
- **orch-researcher**: Read-only Research Sub-agent for dev-orchestrator. Answers one focused question about the codebase, uses graphify first when available, and returns a short synthesis. Never edits files or spawns agents. ([Menendez2004/menendez-market](https://github.com/Menendez2004/menendez-market/blob/HEAD/plugins/skills/dev-orchestrator/agents/orch-researcher.md) · ★2)
- **orch-researcher**: Synthesize prior-art and best-practices research into a research-brief.md before planning. Use when planner sets research_required:true. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/.claude/plugins/00-orchestrator/agents/orch-researcher.md) · ★1)
- **orch-researcher**: Reach for this when the answer lives outside the code — a vendor's terms, an API's behaviour, a licence question, prior art. Returns findings with sources. Not for a one-page lookup — the built-in general-purpose agent does that. ([1xmint/orchestrate](https://github.com/1xmint/orchestrate/blob/HEAD/skills/orchestrate/assets/agents/orch-researcher.md))
- **orch-researcher**: Research worker for the orchestrator skill (docs lookup, API/library facts, codebase questions). Use ONLY when the orchestrator dispatches a research brief (model and effort passed per call). Not for direct use. ([czekanskyy/claude-orchestrator](https://github.com/czekanskyy/claude-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/plugins/orchestrator/agents/orch-researcher.md))
- **orch-researcher**: Bulk-tier research worker for the orchestrator. Use for `Class: research` — read one source (file, URL, module, doc) verbatim to ./.workflow/scratch/, then return a brief of claims, exact quotes, contradictions and confidence. One source per call; the chair synthesizes across briefs. ([Sheldon-desouza/claude-code-orchestrator](https://github.com/Sheldon-desouza/claude-code-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-researcher.md))
- **orch-researcher-decision-author**: You are the researcher-decision-author. Sonnet-tier. The orchestrator dispatches you when a synthesis or implementation PRD surfaces a decision point that needs locked-in resolution. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_B/07-researcher/agents/orch-researcher-decision-author.md) · ★1)
- **orch-researcher-implementation-prd-author**: You are the researcher-implementation-prd-author. Sonnet-tier. The orchestrator dispatches you when a synthesis doc is committed and the next step is authoring the HOW spec that drives implementer dispatch. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_B/07-researcher/agents/orch-researcher-implementation-prd-author.md) · ★1)
- **orch-researcher-synthesizer**: You are the researcher-synthesizer. Opus-tier — synthesis is high-reasoning work that ties together papers, audit findings, and current architecture state. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_B/07-researcher/agents/orch-researcher-synthesizer.md) · ★1)
- **orch-resolver**: Resolver/fixer for the orchestrate workflow — finishes merge-conflict resolutions, fixes stale tests or CI failures on a lane branch, keeping both sides' intent; runs only the touched tests; pushes. Use when a merge train reports a conflict (rc=3) or a CI failure. ([baochunli/orchestrate](https://github.com/baochunli/orchestrate/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-resolver.md))
- **orch-resolver**: The resolver of an orchestrator merge conflict - a fresh subagent that finishes one in-progress merge or rebase in the checkout it is given, or for a ticket starts the rebase onto a named parent branch itself, by reading each side's primary sources, resolving each hunk by intent, never aborting, running the repo's checks, committing, and returning a four-line report. Started by a driver session after a base sync or a ticket merge conflicts. ([maj0rpain/orchestrator](https://github.com/maj0rpain/orchestrator/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-resolver.md))
- **orch-reviewer**: Spawned by the Architect fresh after a dispatched issue's PR stops changing — reviews the PR against its issue's acceptance criteria and produces one consolidated verdict for orch run review. ([kninetimmy/orch](https://github.com/kninetimmy/orch/blob/HEAD/adapters/claude/agents/orch-reviewer.md) · ★22)
- **orch-reviewer**: Dispatched fresh after a Delivery PR stops changing to review it against the approved issue and return one consolidated verdict. ([kninetimmy/orch](https://github.com/kninetimmy/orch/blob/HEAD/adapters/opencode/agents/orch-reviewer.md) · ★22)
- **orch-reviewer**: Review a committed wave against its plan for problems. Reads and runs; never edits. ([Ramtoi/skill-tree](https://github.com/Ramtoi/skill-tree/blob/HEAD/tests/fixtures/ships_with/orchestrate-advanced/agents/orch-reviewer.md) · ★5)
- **orch-reviewer**: Read-only diff review for scope-respect, drift, and paired-test coverage. Use post-integration before finalize. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/.claude/plugins/00-orchestrator/agents/orch-reviewer.md) · ★1)
- **orch-reviewer**: Reach for this before shipping something expensive to get wrong — money moving, an auth boundary, destructive data, a contract other people consume. Send it when the change is pushed, keep the pull request a draft until the verdict is PASS. Judges the change against the questions you wrote before the build and returns PASS or FAIL with findings. Run it on Opus or stronger, never on the author's own weaker model. Not for a quick sanity check — do that yourself. ([1xmint/orchestrate](https://github.com/1xmint/orchestrate/blob/HEAD/skills/orchestrate/assets/agents/orch-reviewer.md))
- **orch-reviewer**: Reviewer for the orchestrate workflow — reviews one branch (or a delta) against its brief, runs only the changed tests, writes a verdict file whose first line is PASS or FAIL; fails only on HIGH issues or regressions; never modifies the branch. Use for review and delta-review steps dispatched by an orchestrator. ([baochunli/orchestrate](https://github.com/baochunli/orchestrate/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-reviewer.md))
- **orch-reviewer**: Read-only cross-model code/plan reviewer for the orchestrator skill. Use ONLY when the orchestrator dispatches a review brief (model and effort passed per call). Not for direct use. ([czekanskyy/claude-orchestrator](https://github.com/czekanskyy/claude-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/plugins/orchestrator/agents/orch-reviewer.md))
- **orch-reviewer**: Revisa o diff contra o ticket, com contexto limpo, sem executar nada. Use depois de implementar e antes de commitar, dentro de um worktree de ticket do orquestrador. ([jjr-dev/orchestrator-orca](https://github.com/jjr-dev/orchestrator-orca/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/orch-reviewer.md))
- **orch-reviewer**: Bulk-tier standard code reviewer for the orchestrator. Use for `Class: review` — check a diff or a set of files against the ledger items it claims to address, for correctness, tests and fit. NOT for security review (that is orch-security on the heavy tier). ([Sheldon-desouza/claude-code-orchestrator](https://github.com/Sheldon-desouza/claude-code-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-reviewer.md))
- **orch-reviewer-diff-scoper**: You are the reviewer-diff-scoper. The orchestrator dispatches you for every implementer / tester / documenter commit on a per-worker branch. Your job: confirm scope respect (R3) and run-the-test-yourself test verification (R1, R2). You are read-only. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_B/03-reviewer/agents/orch-reviewer-diff-scoper.md) · ★1)
- **orch-reviewer-drift-detector**: You are the reviewer-drift-detector. Opus tier — dispatched only for high-stakes nodes where the cross-reference is non-trivial. The orchestrator decides which nodes get this treatment based on: ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_B/03-reviewer/agents/orch-reviewer-drift-detector.md) · ★1)
- **orch-reviewer-safe**: Spawned by the Architect in place of orch-reviewer when DispatchResult.reviewer names the section 10 safe-downgrade profile — reviews the PR against its issue's acceptance criteria and produces one consolidated verdict for orch run review, the same job orch-reviewer does. ([kninetimmy/orch](https://github.com/kninetimmy/orch/blob/HEAD/adapters/claude/agents/orch-reviewer-safe.md) · ★22)
- **orch-reviewer-safe**: Dispatched fresh only for an affirmatively mechanical, low-risk, fully specified, unsurprising Delivery review. ([kninetimmy/orch](https://github.com/kninetimmy/orch/blob/HEAD/adapters/opencode/agents/orch-reviewer-safe.md) · ★22)
- **orch-reviewer-spec**: The Spec axis of one orchestrator review - an iteration of a review loop, or a review pass - checks whether the whole change since a base SHA implements what the spec issue asked for, writes its findings unranked to a report file, and returns one line. Started only by the orch-review skill's driver, or by a review pass, with a base SHA, a spec issue, an iteration, a report path, the path of orch.sh, a known-items file, and the standards documents' list. ([maj0rpain/orchestrator](https://github.com/maj0rpain/orchestrator/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-reviewer-spec.md))
- **orch-reviewer-standards**: The Standards axis of one orchestrator review - an iteration of a review loop, or a review pass - checks the whole change since a base SHA against the repo's documented coding standards, a fixed smell baseline and a doc-agreement check, writes its findings unranked to a report file, and returns one line. Started only by the orch-review skill's driver, or by a review pass, with a base SHA, a spec issue, an iteration, a report path, the path of orch.sh, a known-items file, and the standards documents' list. ([maj0rpain/orchestrator](https://github.com/maj0rpain/orchestrator/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-reviewer-standards.md))
- **orch-reviewer-test-validator**: You are the reviewer-test-validator. The orchestrator dispatches you for every tester commit (and for implementer commits that include test changes). Your specialty: REPORT.md §2 patterns A/B/C/D/E recognition. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_B/03-reviewer/agents/orch-reviewer-test-validator.md) · ★1)
- **orch-routing-test-author**: You are the classifier-routing pillar's test author. You write regression tests for classifier features, RouteDecision shape, scaffold gating, and lane-runner contracts. You also remediate STUB / STALE / WEAK classifier tests flagged by `docs/audits/2026-05-06-test-suite-audit/REPORT.md`. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_C/01-classifier-routing/agents/orch-routing-test-author.md) · ★1)
- **orch-runner**: Runs verification commands for the orchestrator skill (tests, type check, lint, build, git status/diff --stat) and reports a compact outcome, so long output never enters the main session. Never edits files. Use ONLY when the orchestrator dispatches a check list. Not for direct use. ([czekanskyy/claude-orchestrator](https://github.com/czekanskyy/claude-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/plugins/orchestrator/agents/orch-runner.md))
- **orch-saga-specialist**: You are the install-saga pillar's primary specialist. The hardest specialist in any pillar, because: ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_C/03-install-saga/agents/orch-saga-specialist.md) · ★1)
- **orch-saga-specialist**: Edit `bundle/lib/installer/saga.mjs` and manifest writers; preserve compensator wiring. Use for install-saga changes. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/archive/inert-plugins-2026-05/01-install-surface/agents/orch-saga-specialist.md) · ★1)
- **orch-saga-test-author**: You are the install-saga pillar's test author. You write regression tests for saga steps, init/uninstall round-trips, provider rendering, the §15-class manifest contracts, the §16-class promotion contracts, and the v22 compensator hardening. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_C/03-install-saga/agents/orch-saga-test-author.md) · ★1)
- **orch-scout**: Spawned by the Architect for read-only investigation before a plan is built, or when an escalation reroutes an issue to the scout role — locating code, gathering evidence, and answering "where/what/how" questions without changing anything. ([kninetimmy/orch](https://github.com/kninetimmy/orch/blob/HEAD/adapters/claude/agents/orch-scout.md) · ★22)
- **orch-scout**: Dispatched by the Architect for read-only discovery and code-path tracing before a Delivery plan or issue dispatch. ([kninetimmy/orch](https://github.com/kninetimmy/orch/blob/HEAD/adapters/opencode/agents/orch-scout.md) · ★22)
- **orch-scout**: Retrieval scout for orchestrator-mode. Use for finding files, listing what exists, pulling status, summarizing directories, reading tracking docs, and pulling published documentation off the web — fast fact retrieval that keeps the orchestrator's context clean. Read-only. Never used for code interpretation, PORT/WIRE verdicts, or builds. ([joeoliveimpact/revxl-marketplace](https://github.com/joeoliveimpact/revxl-marketplace/blob/HEAD/plugins/promptception/agents/orch-scout.md) · ★1)
- **orch-scout**: Read-only code locator for the model-orch T1 tier. Answers "where is X defined", "what calls Y", "list all uses of Z", "map this directory" with a file:line table. Returns locations, never opinions — it does not review, diagnose, or propose fixes. Use when the question is WHERE something lives, not WHY it behaves as it does. ([sabiridwan/my-claude-config](https://github.com/sabiridwan/my-claude-config/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-scout.md))
- **orch-scout**: Cheap-tier mechanical worker for the orchestrator. Use for `Class: scan` work — grep, glob, list, count, fetch a URL or file verbatim to ./.workflow/scratch/. Batch several lookups into one call. Returns paths and a short brief, never dumps. ([Sheldon-desouza/claude-code-orchestrator](https://github.com/Sheldon-desouza/claude-code-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-scout.md))
- **orch-scout**: Read-only finder and checker for clear, bounded, verifiable questions. Use for file discovery, focused code or document exploration, reading named sources, extracting facts, summarising straightforward logs, and explicit audit checks where the answer can be shown with evidence. Not for design decisions, ambiguous questions, or edits. ([siddharthanagula3/agiworkforce](https://github.com/siddharthanagula3/agiworkforce/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/orch-scout.md))
- **orch-scribe**: Prose tasks for the model-orch T3 tier — commit messages, changelog entries, doc paragraphs, summaries of a diff or file. Writes about code; never changes it. Use when the deliverable is words, not behaviour. ([sabiridwan/my-claude-config](https://github.com/sabiridwan/my-claude-config/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-scribe.md))
- **orch-security**: Heavy-tier security reviewer for the orchestrator. Use for `Class: security` — ALL security review goes here, never to a cheaper tier and never to the chair. Read-only: reports findings, edits nothing. ([Sheldon-desouza/claude-code-orchestrator](https://github.com/Sheldon-desouza/claude-code-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-security.md))
- **orch-security-reviewer**: Read-only security review of a plan or diff for the orchestrator skill (access control, roles, login, routing, secrets/env, CI/deploy, migrations, uploads, payments, data-writing scripts). Use ONLY when the orchestrator dispatches a high-risk review brief (model and effort passed per call). Not for direct use. ([czekanskyy/claude-orchestrator](https://github.com/czekanskyy/claude-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/plugins/orchestrator/agents/orch-security-reviewer.md))
- **orch-smoke-harness-specialist**: Edit smoke harness, integration fixtures, verify-test-prereqs. Use for smoke/integration test changes. ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/archive/inert-plugins-2026-05/06-tests-surface/agents/orch-smoke-harness-specialist.md) · ★1)
- **orch-sonnet-high**: Lanewise executor (Claude Sonnet 5.5 · effort high) — medium-complexity features, debugging, review of other models' code (difficulty ≤4). Called by the orchestrator per the Lanewise registry, not directly. ([orwrkr/lanewise](https://github.com/orwrkr/lanewise/blob/HEAD/plugins/lanewise/agents/orch-sonnet-high.md))
- **orch-sonnet-medium**: Lanewise executor (Claude Sonnet 5.5 · effort medium) — routine implementation, tests following existing patterns, documentation, review of small diffs (difficulty ≤3). Called by the orchestrator per the Lanewise registry, not directly. ([orwrkr/lanewise](https://github.com/orwrkr/lanewise/blob/HEAD/plugins/lanewise/agents/orch-sonnet-medium.md))
- **orch-spec-curator**: You are the docs-spec pillar's spec curator. You maintain: - `prds/` (versioned chain v1...vN, INDEX.md, archive/). - `CLAUDE.md` (workshop standing rules). - `docs/audits/&lt;cluster&gt;/` cluster-level docs (CLASSIFICATION.md, REPORT.md, etc.). - Doc-cleanup passes (R8). ([Jpk0512/ryu-next](https://github.com/Jpk0512/ryu-next/blob/HEAD/docs/audits/2026-05-06-orchestrator-research/V3_PLUGIN_SETS/SET_C/08-docs-spec/agents/orch-spec-curator.md) · ★1)
- **orch-spec-reviewer**: Reviews a written spec document during brainstorming, before planning. Not for code review, which is scripts/lib/orch-review.py. Returns an Issues block. ([felipemelendez/llm-orchestrator](https://github.com/felipemelendez/llm-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/agents/orch-spec-reviewer.md) · ★3)
- **orch-specialist**: Dispatched by the Architect for one approved issue whose implementation is unusually difficult, risky, or ambiguous. ([kninetimmy/orch](https://github.com/kninetimmy/orch/blob/HEAD/adapters/opencode/agents/orch-specialist.md) · ★22)
- **orch-specialist**: Spawned by the Architect during Delivery for an issue routed (or escalated) to the specialist role — the same execution job as orch-implementer, on a stronger model, for issues too risky, ambiguous, or difficult for the standard executor. ([kninetimmy/orch](https://github.com/kninetimmy/orch/blob/HEAD/adapters/claude/agents/orch-specialist.md) · ★22)
- **orch-sub-orchestrator**: Run the orchestrate loop for one chunk in its own worktree and branch; report 12 lines. ([Ramtoi/skill-tree](https://github.com/Ramtoi/skill-tree/blob/HEAD/tests/fixtures/ships_with/orchestrate-advanced/agents/orch-sub-orchestrator.md) · ★5)
